I’m here because people keep acting like what’s happening with my house is just normal paperwork, like it’s just about missed payments or some bank process, but that’s not what it is. I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together to push me out, and it’s not just them. There are people at my job who have been involved too, quietly, behind the scenes.

This has been going on for almost two years now, and every time I try to explain it, someone pulls out a document or says, “See, this is just foreclosure paperwork,” like I’m supposed to ignore everything I’ve been seeing. But papers can be made to look official. That doesn’t change the way the timing lines up, the way people act around me, the little comments, the way things keep happening one after another. I came in because I’m tired of everyone pretending this is some simple financial issue when I know there’s more going on.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I can’t say every stranger on the street is involved, if that’s what you’re asking. I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. But it doesn’t feel like it’s only one person at the bank pushing a button somewhere, either.

That’s the part everyone keeps trying to explain away with paperwork. They show me forms and notices and say it’s just missed payments or normal foreclosure steps, but I’ve been watching how this has unfolded for almost two years. The timing, the way things happen right after I speak up, the way people act like they already know something before I’ve even said anything—it’s not normal.

So are there outside groups? I don’t know how to name it like that. I just know it doesn’t feel contained to one office or one department. It feels coordinated. And when people keep insisting it’s all just routine paperwork, that honestly makes me more sure they’re trying to make me ignore what’s right in front of me.

No… I don’t think so. Not like thoughts are being put into my head, or like somebody has a remote control over my mind or anything like that.

I mean, I’ve had people try to influence me, definitely—pressure me, confuse me, make me second-guess what I know I saw. That’s different, though. That’s not someone controlling my mind. My thoughts feel like mine. I may go over things a lot, and I may keep replaying conversations or paperwork or the timing of things because I’m trying to make sense of it, but it still feels like me thinking it through.

So, no, I haven’t noticed anything like thoughts being inserted or someone taking over my mind. That’s not really how I would describe what’s been happening.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. I mean, that’s exactly part of what I’ve been trying to explain, and people keep making it sound like I’m reading too much into things. But when comments happen at the exact wrong time—or honestly, the exact right time, if you understand what I mean—it doesn’t feel random.

Like with the house, everyone keeps saying foreclosure like that word explains everything. They act like it’s just missed payments and forms and notices, but I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together to push me out. And then certain people at my job… they’ll say something small, something that could sound normal to someone else, but I can tell it’s connected. It’ll be right after I’ve made a call, or after I’ve questioned something, or after some new paperwork shows up. They won’t say it directly, of course. That’s the whole point. It’s always indirect enough that if I repeat it, people can say, “Oh, they didn’t mean anything by that.”

But I’ve been living with this for almost two years. I’m the one seeing the pattern. I’m the one noticing how things line up. So yes, sometimes comments or little actions do feel like they’re meant for me, like a signal or a reminder that they know what’s going on and they’re still moving forward with it. Other people might not see it, but they’re not the ones it’s directed at.

Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you believe that people who aren’t directly involved in the bank or your job are also part of this situation—like strangers, organizations, or groups working against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can’t say every stranger on the street is involved, if that’s what you’re asking. I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. But it doesn’t feel like it’s only one person at the bank pushing a button somewhere, either.\n\nWhat I know is that the bank, the loan servicer, and certain people at my job have been moving in the same direction against me, quietly. That’s the part everyone keeps trying to explain away with paperwork. They show me forms and notices and say it’s just missed payments or normal foreclosure steps, but I’ve been watching how this has unfolded for almost two years. The timing, the way things happen right after I speak up, the way people act like they already know something before I’ve even said anything—it’s not normal.\n\nSo are there outside groups? I don’t know how to name it like that. I just know it doesn’t feel contained to one office or one department. It feels coordinated. And when people keep insisting it’s all just routine paperwork, that honestly makes me more sure they’re trying to make me ignore what’s right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like someone is putting thoughts into your head or controlling your mind in some way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like thoughts are being put into my head, or like somebody has a remote control over my mind or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had people try to influence me, definitely—pressure me, confuse me, make me second-guess what I know I saw. That’s different, though. That’s not someone controlling my mind. My thoughts feel like mine. I may go over things a lot, and I may keep replaying conversations or paperwork or the timing of things because I’m trying to make sense of it, but it still feels like me thinking it through.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed anything like thoughts being inserted or someone taking over my mind. That’s not really how I would describe what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain events, comments, or actions by others have a special or hidden meaning that’s directed specifically at you, even if others might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, that’s exactly part of what I’ve been trying to explain, and people keep making it sound like I’m reading too much into things. But when comments happen at the exact wrong time—or honestly, the exact right time, if you understand what I mean—it doesn’t feel random.\n\nLike with the house, everyone keeps saying foreclosure like that word explains everything. They act like it’s just missed payments and forms and notices, but I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together to push me out. And then certain people at my job… they’ll say something small, something that could sound normal to someone else, but I can tell it’s connected. It’ll be right after I’ve made a call, or after I’ve questioned something, or after some new paperwork shows up. They won’t say it directly, of course. That’s the whole point. It’s always indirect enough that if I repeat it, people can say, “Oh, they didn’t mean anything by that.”\n\nBut I’ve been living with this for almost two years. I’m the one seeing the pattern. I’m the one noticing how things line up. So yes, sometimes comments or little actions do feel like they’re meant for me, like a signal or a reminder that they know what’s going on and they’re still moving forward with it. Other people might not see it, but they’re not the ones it’s directed at.\n\n[Last Question]\nOutside of what’s happening with your house and job, have you noticed events, comments, or actions by others that you felt had a special meaning directed at you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 36 / first_line_supervisor_of_retail_sales_worker / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house, and no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside of the house and my job? I mean… that’s hard to separate, because the house and the job are where most of it shows up. That’s where the pressure is coming from, or at least that’s where I can actually point to things and say, “This happened, then that happened.” I’m not saying every random person or every little thing in the world is about me.

But I’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me another letter or another form that’s supposed to erase what I’ve been seeing. And it doesn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, and even certain people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. That’s what I keep trying to explain.

So if you mean do I think, like, a billboard or a stranger’s comment at the store is sending me a message—no, not really, not in that way. But the things connected to the foreclosure, the calls, the notices, the way people at work act or say things around the same time—that has meaning. It’s directed at me. I’ve been watching it for almost two years, and everyone keeps reducing it to “routine process,” but that’s not what it feels like when you’re the one living through it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really outside of that, no. I mean, I keep trying to say it’s not like I think everything in the world is aimed at me. It’s not strangers on the street or the news or something like that. It’s specifically what’s been happening with the house, and then how certain people at work seem tied into it.

I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough letters or forms then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I imagined the rest of it.” But I didn’t. I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together, quietly, and people at my job have been involved too, to push me out of my house. And this has been going on for almost two years, so it’s not one comment or one notice that I misunderstood.

So if you’re asking about things totally unrelated to my house or my job, no, I don’t really have examples of that. But I also don’t think you can separate everything so neatly, because the pressure around the house shows up in different places. That’s the part people keep ignoring.

That’s where the pressure is coming from, or at least that’s where I can actually point to things and say, “This happened, then that happened.” I’m not saying every random person or every little thing in the world is about me.\n\nBut I’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me another letter or another form that’s supposed to erase what I’ve been seeing. And it doesn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, and even certain people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. That’s what I keep trying to explain.\n\nSo if you mean do I think, like, a billboard or a stranger’s comment at the store is sending me a message—no, not really, not in that way. I’ve been watching it for almost two years, and everyone keeps reducing it to “routine process,” but that’s not what it feels like when you’re the one living through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or comments not related to your house or job were directed at you or had a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. I mean, I keep trying to say it’s not like I think everything in the world is aimed at me. It’s not strangers on the street or the news or something like that. It’s specifically what’s been happening with the house, and then how certain people at work seem tied into it.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough letters or forms then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I imagined the rest of it.” But I didn’t. I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together, quietly, and people at my job have been involved too, to push me out of my house. And this has been going on for almost two years, so it’s not one comment or one notice that I misunderstood.\n\nSo if you’re asking about things totally unrelated to my house or my job, no, I don’t really have examples of that. But I also don’t think you can separate everything so neatly, because the pressure around the house shows up in different places. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not like TV or radio sending me coded messages, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking they’re talking about me personally, or that some online ad is secretly meant for me. That’s not really what this is.

What I’m saying is much more specific, and I feel like that keeps getting blurred. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s all neutral and ordinary and if they just show me enough documents, I’m supposed to accept that nothing else is happening. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s where the messages are, if you want to call them that—the timing of calls, notices, comments at work, things lining up in a way that’s not just coincidence.

So no, I wouldn’t say public media is communicating about me. But the people involved in the house situation, yes, absolutely. And no amount of papers they put in front of me changes what I’ve watched happen for almost two years.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s really only come up around the house and then the job because of how the job got mixed into it. I’m not saying this is some lifelong thing where I’ve always thought people were sending me signs or targeting me in every part of my life. That’s not how it started, and that’s not how I experience it.

But with this foreclosure, everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just paperwork, like missed payments and standard notices and forms, and if they show me enough pages then I’m supposed to ignore the rest of what’s been happening. I can’t do that. I know what I’ve been watching for almost two years. The bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. It’s the timing, the way things get said, the way the pressure builds from different directions and then everyone acts like it’s unrelated.

So no, not other areas before this. It’s this situation. But it’s not “just” the house in the simple way people keep saying, because once my job became part of it, it started affecting everything around me.

Other people might not see it, but they’re not the ones it’s directed at.\n\nDoctor: Outside of what’s happening with your house and job, have you noticed events, comments, or actions by others that you felt had a special meaning directed at you?\n\nPatient: Outside of the house and my job? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not outside of that. I mean, I know how it sounds when I say the bank and the loan servicer and people at my job are all connected in this, because people look at me like I’m making some huge leap. But to me that is not “impossible.” That’s the part I keep trying to explain.

I don’t believe in things that can’t happen, like, I don’t think there are supernatural forces or secret messages from the TV or anything like that. This is about real people and real institutions that have power over my life. Banks work with servicers. Employers talk to people. People can coordinate quietly without putting it in writing. That’s not impossible.

What upsets me is that everyone keeps reducing it to foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, like if they stack enough documents in front of me then I’m supposed to pretend I didn’t notice the timing, the pressure, the way certain things at work started lining up with what was happening with the house. I’ve been seeing this for almost two years. So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve believed something completely outside reality. I think other people just don’t want to admit what’s happening here.

It’s always indirect enough that if I repeat it, people can say, “Oh, they didn’t mean anything by that.”\n\nBut I’ve been living with this for almost two years. I’m the one seeing the pattern. I’m the one noticing how things line up. So yes, sometimes comments or little actions do feel like they’re meant for me, like a signal or a reminder that they know what’s going on and they’re still moving forward with it. Other people might not see it, but they’re not the ones it’s directed at.\n\nDoctor: Outside of what’s happening with your house and job, have you noticed events, comments, or actions by others that you felt had a special meaning directed at you?\n\nPatient: Outside of the house and my job? I mean… that’s hard to separate, because the house and the job are where most of it shows up. That’s where the pressure is coming from, or at least that’s where I can actually point to things and say, “This happened, then that happened.” I’m not saying every random person or every little thing in the world is about me.\n\nBut I’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me another letter or another form that’s supposed to erase what I’ve been seeing. And it doesn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, and even certain people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. That’s what I keep trying to explain.\n\nSo if you mean do I think, like, a billboard or a stranger’s comment at the store is sending me a message—no, not really, not in that way. But the things connected to the foreclosure, the calls, the notices, the way people at work act or say things around the same time—that has meaning. It’s directed at me. I’ve been watching it for almost two years, and everyone keeps reducing it to “routine process,” but that’s not what it feels like when you’re the one living through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or comments not related to your house or job were directed at you or had a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. I mean, I keep trying to say it’s not like I think everything in the world is aimed at me. It’s not strangers on the street or the news or something like that. It’s specifically what’s been happening with the house, and then how certain people at work seem tied into it.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough letters or forms then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I imagined the rest of it.” But I didn’t. I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together, quietly, and people at my job have been involved too, to push me out of my house. And this has been going on for almost two years, so it’s not one comment or one notice that I misunderstood.\n\nSo if you’re asking about things totally unrelated to my house or my job, no, I don’t really have examples of that. But I also don’t think you can separate everything so neatly, because the pressure around the house shows up in different places. That’s the part people keep ignoring.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that media, technology, or public messages (like on TV, radio, or online) were being used to send you hidden messages or communicate about you in a way that others wouldn't notice?\n\nPatient: No, not like TV or radio sending me coded messages, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking they’re talking about me personally, or that some online ad is secretly meant for me. That’s not really what this is.\n\nWhat I’m saying is much more specific, and I feel like that keeps getting blurred. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s all neutral and ordinary and if they just show me enough documents, I’m supposed to accept that nothing else is happening. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s where the messages are, if you want to call them that—the timing of calls, notices, comments at work, things lining up in a way that’s not just coincidence.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say public media is communicating about me. But the people involved in the house situation, yes, absolutely. And no amount of papers they put in front of me changes what I’ve watched happen for almost two years.\n\nDoctor: Has this pattern of feeling targeted or receiving hidden messages happened in other areas of your life before, or is this something that’s only come up around the situation with your house and job?\n\nPatient: It’s really only come up around the house and then the job because of how the job got mixed into it. I’m not saying this is some lifelong thing where I’ve always thought people were sending me signs or targeting me in every part of my life. That’s not how it started, and that’s not how I experience it.\n\nBut with this foreclosure, everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just paperwork, like missed payments and standard notices and forms, and if they show me enough pages then I’m supposed to ignore the rest of what’s been happening. I can’t do that. I know what I’ve been watching for almost two years. The bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. It’s the timing, the way things get said, the way the pressure builds from different directions and then everyone acts like it’s unrelated.\n\nSo no, not other areas before this. It’s this situation. But it’s not “just” the house in the simple way people keep saying, because once my job became part of it, it started affecting everything around me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that others might consider completely impossible or not based in reality at all, even outside of what’s happening with your house and job?\n\nPatient: No, not outside of that. I mean, I know how it sounds when I say the bank and the loan servicer and people at my job are all connected in this, because people look at me like I’m making some huge leap. But to me that is not “impossible.” That’s the part I keep trying to explain.\n\nI don’t believe in things that can’t happen, like, I don’t think there are supernatural forces or secret messages from the TV or anything like that. This is about real people and real institutions that have power over my life. Banks work with servicers. Employers talk to people. People can coordinate quietly without putting it in writing. That’s not impossible.\n\nWhat upsets me is that everyone keeps reducing it to foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, like if they stack enough documents in front of me then I’m supposed to pretend I didn’t notice the timing, the pressure, the way certain things at work started lining up with what was happening with the house. I’ve been seeing this for almost two years. So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve believed something completely outside reality. I think other people just don’t want to admit what’s happening here.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But to me that is not “impossible.” That’s the part I keep trying to explain.\n\nI don’t believe in things that can’t happen, like, I don’t think there are supernatural forces or secret messages from the TV or anything like that. This is about real people and real institutions that have power over my life. Banks work with servicers. Employers talk to people. People can coordinate quietly without putting it in writing. That’s not impossible.\n\nWhat upsets me is that everyone keeps reducing it to foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, like if they stack enough documents in front of me then I’m supposed to pretend I didn’t notice the timing, the pressure, the way certain things at work started lining up with what was happening with the house. I’ve been seeing this for almost two years. So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve believed something completely outside reality. I think other people just don’t want to admit what’s happening here.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had beliefs about yourself or the world that were not shared by others and that you thought were true even when there was strong evidence against them, outside of what’s been happening with your house and job?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 36 / first_line_supervisor_of_retail_sales_worker / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house, and no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside of the house and work situation? No, I don’t think so. That’s what I keep trying to separate, because it feels like everyone wants to make this into something bigger or stranger than it is, like I just go around believing things nobody else believes no matter what. I don’t.

This is about the foreclosure. It’s about everyone acting like it’s only paperwork and missed payments and “standard process,” and then expecting me to just accept that because they put documents in front of me. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I know that sounds like something other people don’t want to agree with, but I’ve been watching it happen for almost two years.

So if you mean have I had beliefs like that in some totally different area of my life, no. I’m not saying the whole world is against me or that everything has hidden meaning. But with this? No amount of papers changes what I’ve seen. The timing, the pressure from different directions, the way people at work started acting around the same time as the foreclosure things were happening—it all fits together too much to be coincidence.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because people keep acting like what’s happening with my house is just normal paperwork, like it’s just about missed payments or some bank process, but that’s not what it is. I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together to push me out, and it’s not just them. There are people at my job who have been involved too, quietly, behind the scenes.\n\nThis has been going on for almost two years now, and every time I try to explain it, someone pulls out a document or says, “See, this is just foreclosure paperwork,” like I’m supposed to ignore everything I’ve been seeing. But papers can be made to look official. That doesn’t change the way the timing lines up, the way people act around me, the little comments, the way things keep happening one after another. I came in because I’m tired of everyone pretending this is some simple financial issue when I know there’s more going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people who aren’t directly involved in the bank or your job are also part of this situation—like strangers, organizations, or groups working against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can’t say every stranger on the street is involved, if that’s what you’re asking. I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. But it doesn’t feel like it’s only one person at the bank pushing a button somewhere, either.\n\nWhat I know is that the bank, the loan servicer, and certain people at my job have been moving in the same direction against me, quietly. That’s the part everyone keeps trying to explain away with paperwork. They show me forms and notices and say it’s just missed payments or normal foreclosure steps, but I’ve been watching how this has unfolded for almost two years. The timing, the way things happen right after I speak up, the way people act like they already know something before I’ve even said anything—it’s not normal.\n\nSo are there outside groups? I don’t know how to name it like that. I just know it doesn’t feel contained to one office or one department. It feels coordinated. And when people keep insisting it’s all just routine paperwork, that honestly makes me more sure they’re trying to make me ignore what’s right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like someone is putting thoughts into your head or controlling your mind in some way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like thoughts are being put into my head, or like somebody has a remote control over my mind or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had people try to influence me, definitely—pressure me, confuse me, make me second-guess what I know I saw. That’s different, though. That’s not someone controlling my mind. My thoughts feel like mine. I may go over things a lot, and I may keep replaying conversations or paperwork or the timing of things because I’m trying to make sense of it, but it still feels like me thinking it through.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed anything like thoughts being inserted or someone taking over my mind. That’s not really how I would describe what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain events, comments, or actions by others have a special or hidden meaning that’s directed specifically at you, even if others might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, that’s exactly part of what I’ve been trying to explain, and people keep making it sound like I’m reading too much into things. But when comments happen at the exact wrong time—or honestly, the exact right time, if you understand what I mean—it doesn’t feel random.\n\nLike with the house, everyone keeps saying foreclosure like that word explains everything. They act like it’s just missed payments and forms and notices, but I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together to push me out. And then certain people at my job… they’ll say something small, something that could sound normal to someone else, but I can tell it’s connected. It’ll be right after I’ve made a call, or after I’ve questioned something, or after some new paperwork shows up. They won’t say it directly, of course. That’s the whole point. It’s always indirect enough that if I repeat it, people can say, “Oh, they didn’t mean anything by that.”\n\nBut I’ve been living with this for almost two years. I’m the one seeing the pattern. I’m the one noticing how things line up. So yes, sometimes comments or little actions do feel like they’re meant for me, like a signal or a reminder that they know what’s going on and they’re still moving forward with it. Other people might not see it, but they’re not the ones it’s directed at.\n\nDoctor: Outside of what’s happening with your house and job, have you noticed events, comments, or actions by others that you felt had a special meaning directed at you?\n\nPatient: Outside of the house and my job? I mean… that’s hard to separate, because the house and the job are where most of it shows up. That’s where the pressure is coming from, or at least that’s where I can actually point to things and say, “This happened, then that happened.” I’m not saying every random person or every little thing in the world is about me.\n\nBut I’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me another letter or another form that’s supposed to erase what I’ve been seeing. And it doesn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, and even certain people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. That’s what I keep trying to explain.\n\nSo if you mean do I think, like, a billboard or a stranger’s comment at the store is sending me a message—no, not really, not in that way. But the things connected to the foreclosure, the calls, the notices, the way people at work act or say things around the same time—that has meaning. It’s directed at me. I’ve been watching it for almost two years, and everyone keeps reducing it to “routine process,” but that’s not what it feels like when you’re the one living through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or comments not related to your house or job were directed at you or had a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. I mean, I keep trying to say it’s not like I think everything in the world is aimed at me. It’s not strangers on the street or the news or something like that. It’s specifically what’s been happening with the house, and then how certain people at work seem tied into it.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough letters or forms then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I imagined the rest of it.” But I didn’t. I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together, quietly, and people at my job have been involved too, to push me out of my house. And this has been going on for almost two years, so it’s not one comment or one notice that I misunderstood.\n\nSo if you’re asking about things totally unrelated to my house or my job, no, I don’t really have examples of that. But I also don’t think you can separate everything so neatly, because the pressure around the house shows up in different places. That’s the part people keep ignoring.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that media, technology, or public messages (like on TV, radio, or online) were being used to send you hidden messages or communicate about you in a way that others wouldn't notice?\n\nPatient: No, not like TV or radio sending me coded messages, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking they’re talking about me personally, or that some online ad is secretly meant for me. That’s not really what this is.\n\nWhat I’m saying is much more specific, and I feel like that keeps getting blurred. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s all neutral and ordinary and if they just show me enough documents, I’m supposed to accept that nothing else is happening. Yes. I mean, yes, I feel certain. And I know you’re saying “even though others disagree,” but that’s the problem—everyone keeps acting like if enough people disagree with me, or if the bank sends enough paperwork, then that makes what I’m seeing disappear. It doesn’t.

It’s the pattern. That’s the evidence to me. Everyone keeps wanting one single piece of paper, one email, one person admitting it, and I’m saying that’s not how something like this would work. People don’t put “we’re coordinating to push her out” in writing. They use procedures, timing, little pressures from different directions, and then when you point it out they say, “No, that’s just normal.”

I’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if the bank or the servicer shows a notice or a statement then that’s supposed to settle the whole thing. But those papers don’t explain what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. They don’t explain how the pressure from the bank and loan servicer kept lining up with things happening at work, or how people at my job started acting differently right when the house situation got worse. It felt coordinated. It still does.

And I know people say, “Well, the documents show this,” but documents can show one part and hide another. A paper can say a payment was late or a notice was sent, fine, but that doesn’t mean there isn’t something else going on behind it. The bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job all have ways to put pressure on me, and it’s like they’ve been taking turns tightening it until I’m supposed to give up and leave my house.

So my reasoning is not just one thing. It’s the timing, the way things connect, the way explanations are always too neat, and the way everyone keeps trying to make me ignore what I’ve been watching with my own eyes. No amount of paperwork changes that for me.
=====================================

There are people at my job who have been involved too, quietly, behind the scenes.\n\nThis has been going on for almost two years now, and every time I try to explain it, someone pulls out a document or says, “See, this is just foreclosure paperwork,” like I’m supposed to ignore everything I’ve been seeing. But papers can be made to look official. That doesn’t change the way the timing lines up, the way people act around me, the little comments, the way things keep happening one after another. That’s not really how I would describe what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain events, comments, or actions by others have a special or hidden meaning that’s directed specifically at you, even if others might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’m the one noticing how things line up. Other people might not see it, but they’re not the ones it’s directed at.\n\nDoctor: Outside of what’s happening with your house and job, have you noticed events, comments, or actions by others that you felt had a special meaning directed at you?\n\nPatient: Outside of the house and my job? I mean… that’s hard to separate, because the house and the job are where most of it shows up. That’s where the pressure is coming from, or at least that’s where I can actually point to things and say, “This happened, then that happened.” I’m not saying every random person or every little thing in the world is about me.\n\nBut I’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me another letter or another form that’s supposed to erase what I’ve been seeing. And it doesn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, and even certain people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. That’s what I keep trying to explain.\n\nSo if you mean do I think, like, a billboard or a stranger’s comment at the store is sending me a message—no, not really, not in that way. But the things connected to the foreclosure, the calls, the notices, the way people at work act or say things around the same time—that has meaning. It’s directed at me. I’ve been watching it for almost two years, and everyone keeps reducing it to “routine process,” but that’s not what it feels like when you’re the one living through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or comments not related to your house or job were directed at you or had a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. I mean, I keep trying to say it’s not like I think everything in the world is aimed at me. It’s not strangers on the street or the news or something like that. It’s specifically what’s been happening with the house, and then how certain people at work seem tied into it.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough letters or forms then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I imagined the rest of it.” But I didn’t. I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together, quietly, and people at my job have been involved too, to push me out of my house. And this has been going on for almost two years, so it’s not one comment or one notice that I misunderstood.\n\nSo if you’re asking about things totally unrelated to my house or my job, no, I don’t really have examples of that. But I also don’t think you can separate everything so neatly, because the pressure around the house shows up in different places. That’s the part people keep ignoring.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that media, technology, or public messages (like on TV, radio, or online) were being used to send you hidden messages or communicate about you in a way that others wouldn't notice?\n\nPatient: No, not like TV or radio sending me coded messages, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking they’re talking about me personally, or that some online ad is secretly meant for me. That’s not really what this is.\n\nWhat I’m saying is much more specific, and I feel like that keeps getting blurred. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s all neutral and ordinary and if they just show me enough documents, I’m supposed to accept that nothing else is happening. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s where the messages are, if you want to call them that—the timing of calls, notices, comments at work, things lining up in a way that’s not just coincidence.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say public media is communicating about me. But the people involved in the house situation, yes, absolutely. And no amount of papers they put in front of me changes what I’ve watched happen for almost two years.\n\nDoctor: Has this pattern of feeling targeted or receiving hidden messages happened in other areas of your life before, or is this something that’s only come up around the situation with your house and job?\n\nPatient: It’s really only come up around the house and then the job because of how the job got mixed into it. I’m not saying this is some lifelong thing where I’ve always thought people were sending me signs or targeting me in every part of my life. That’s not how it started, and that’s not how I experience it.\n\nBut with this foreclosure, everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just paperwork, like missed payments and standard notices and forms, and if they show me enough pages then I’m supposed to ignore the rest of what’s been happening. I can’t do that. I know what I’ve been watching for almost two years. The bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. It’s the timing, the way things get said, the way the pressure builds from different directions and then everyone acts like it’s unrelated.\n\nSo no, not other areas before this. It’s this situation. But it’s not “just” the house in the simple way people keep saying, because once my job became part of it, it started affecting everything around me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that others might consider completely impossible or not based in reality at all, even outside of what’s happening with your house and job?\n\nPatient: No, not outside of that. I mean, I know how it sounds when I say the bank and the loan servicer and people at my job are all connected in this, because people look at me like I’m making some huge leap. But to me that is not “impossible.” That’s the part I keep trying to explain.\n\nI don’t believe in things that can’t happen, like, I don’t think there are supernatural forces or secret messages from the TV or anything like that. This is about real people and real institutions that have power over my life. Banks work with servicers. Employers talk to people. People can coordinate quietly without putting it in writing. That’s not impossible.\n\nWhat upsets me is that everyone keeps reducing it to foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, like if they stack enough documents in front of me then I’m supposed to pretend I didn’t notice the timing, the pressure, the way certain things at work started lining up with what was happening with the house. I’ve been seeing this for almost two years. So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve believed something completely outside reality. I think other people just don’t want to admit what’s happening here.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs about yourself or the world that were not shared by others and that you thought were true even when there was strong evidence against them, outside of what’s been happening with your house and job?\n\nPatient: Outside of the house and work situation? No, I don’t think so. That’s what I keep trying to separate, because it feels like everyone wants to make this into something bigger or stranger than it is, like I just go around believing things nobody else believes no matter what. I don’t.\n\nThis is about the foreclosure. It’s about everyone acting like it’s only paperwork and missed payments and “standard process,” and then expecting me to just accept that because they put documents in front of me. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I know that sounds like something other people don’t want to agree with, but I’ve been watching it happen for almost two years.\n\nSo if you mean have I had beliefs like that in some totally different area of my life, no. I’m not saying the whole world is against me or that everything has hidden meaning. But with this? No amount of papers changes what I’ve seen. The timing, the pressure from different directions, the way people at work started acting around the same time as the foreclosure things were happening—it all fits together too much to be coincidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel absolutely certain that the bank, the loan servicer, and people at your job have been working together against you, even though others have strongly disagreed or presented evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, yes, I feel certain. And I know you’re saying “even though others disagree,” but that’s the problem—everyone keeps acting like if enough people disagree with me, or if the bank sends enough paperwork, then that makes what I’m seeing disappear. It doesn’t.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps trying to make this foreclosure sound like it’s just missed payments and forms and routine notices, like there’s nothing else going on. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been watching the way it all connects for almost two years. The timing isn’t random. The pressure from the house side and then the pressure at work—it lines up too much.\n\nSo yes, when they show me papers, I look at them, but those papers don’t explain everything. They don’t explain the way people started acting, or how things kept happening at just the right time to corner me. I’m not saying this lightly. I know what I’ve seen, and no amount of documents changes that for me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe any specific evidence or reasoning that has led you to maintain your belief, even when others have shown you paperwork or explanations that suggest otherwise?\n\nPatient: It’s the pattern. That’s the evidence to me. Everyone keeps wanting one single piece of paper, one email, one person admitting it, and I’m saying that’s not how something like this would work. People don’t put “we’re coordinating to push her out” in writing. They use procedures, timing, little pressures from different directions, and then when you point it out they say, “No, that’s just normal.”\n\nI’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if the bank or the servicer shows a notice or a statement then that’s supposed to settle the whole thing. But those papers don’t explain what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking they’re talking about me personally, or that some online ad is secretly meant for me. That’s not really what this is.\n\nWhat I’m saying is much more specific, and I feel like that keeps getting blurred. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s all neutral and ordinary and if they just show me enough documents, I’m supposed to accept that nothing else is happening. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s where the messages are, if you want to call them that—the timing of calls, notices, comments at work, things lining up in a way that’s not just coincidence.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say public media is communicating about me. But the people involved in the house situation, yes, absolutely. And no amount of papers they put in front of me changes what I’ve watched happen for almost two years.\n\nDoctor: Has this pattern of feeling targeted or receiving hidden messages happened in other areas of your life before, or is this something that’s only come up around the situation with your house and job?\n\nPatient: It’s really only come up around the house and then the job because of how the job got mixed into it. I’m not saying this is some lifelong thing where I’ve always thought people were sending me signs or targeting me in every part of my life. That’s not how it started, and that’s not how I experience it.\n\nBut with this foreclosure, everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just paperwork, like missed payments and standard notices and forms, and if they show me enough pages then I’m supposed to ignore the rest of what’s been happening. I can’t do that. I know what I’ve been watching for almost two years. The bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. It’s the timing, the way things get said, the way the pressure builds from different directions and then everyone acts like it’s unrelated.\n\nSo no, not other areas before this. It’s this situation. But it’s not “just” the house in the simple way people keep saying, because once my job became part of it, it started affecting everything around me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that others might consider completely impossible or not based in reality at all, even outside of what’s happening with your house and job?\n\nPatient: No, not outside of that. I mean, I know how it sounds when I say the bank and the loan servicer and people at my job are all connected in this, because people look at me like I’m making some huge leap. But to me that is not “impossible.” That’s the part I keep trying to explain.\n\nI don’t believe in things that can’t happen, like, I don’t think there are supernatural forces or secret messages from the TV or anything like that. This is about real people and real institutions that have power over my life. Banks work with servicers. Employers talk to people. People can coordinate quietly without putting it in writing. That’s not impossible.\n\nWhat upsets me is that everyone keeps reducing it to foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, like if they stack enough documents in front of me then I’m supposed to pretend I didn’t notice the timing, the pressure, the way certain things at work started lining up with what was happening with the house. I’ve been seeing this for almost two years. So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve believed something completely outside reality. I think other people just don’t want to admit what’s happening here.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs about yourself or the world that were not shared by others and that you thought were true even when there was strong evidence against them, outside of what’s been happening with your house and job?\n\nPatient: Outside of the house and work situation? No, I don’t think so. That’s what I keep trying to separate, because it feels like everyone wants to make this into something bigger or stranger than it is, like I just go around believing things nobody else believes no matter what. I don’t.\n\nThis is about the foreclosure. It’s about everyone acting like it’s only paperwork and missed payments and “standard process,” and then expecting me to just accept that because they put documents in front of me. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I know that sounds like something other people don’t want to agree with, but I’ve been watching it happen for almost two years.\n\nSo if you mean have I had beliefs like that in some totally different area of my life, no. I’m not saying the whole world is against me or that everything has hidden meaning. But with this? No amount of papers changes what I’ve seen. The timing, the pressure from different directions, the way people at work started acting around the same time as the foreclosure things were happening—it all fits together too much to be coincidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel absolutely certain that the bank, the loan servicer, and people at your job have been working together against you, even though others have strongly disagreed or presented evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, yes, I feel certain. And I know you’re saying “even though others disagree,” but that’s the problem—everyone keeps acting like if enough people disagree with me, or if the bank sends enough paperwork, then that makes what I’m seeing disappear. It doesn’t.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps trying to make this foreclosure sound like it’s just missed payments and forms and routine notices, like there’s nothing else going on. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been watching the way it all connects for almost two years. The timing isn’t random. The pressure from the house side and then the pressure at work—it lines up too much.\n\nSo yes, when they show me papers, I look at them, but those papers don’t explain everything. They don’t explain the way people started acting, or how things kept happening at just the right time to corner me. I’m not saying this lightly. I know what I’ve seen, and no amount of documents changes that for me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe any specific evidence or reasoning that has led you to maintain your belief, even when others have shown you paperwork or explanations that suggest otherwise?\n\nPatient: It’s the pattern. That’s the evidence to me. Everyone keeps wanting one single piece of paper, one email, one person admitting it, and I’m saying that’s not how something like this would work. People don’t put “we’re coordinating to push her out” in writing. They use procedures, timing, little pressures from different directions, and then when you point it out they say, “No, that’s just normal.”\n\nI’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if the bank or the servicer shows a notice or a statement then that’s supposed to settle the whole thing. But those papers don’t explain what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. They don’t explain how the pressure from the bank and loan servicer kept lining up with things happening at work, or how people at my job started acting differently right when the house situation got worse. It felt coordinated. It still does.\n\nAnd I know people say, “Well, the documents show this,” but documents can show one part and hide another. A paper can say a payment was late or a notice was sent, fine, but that doesn’t mean there isn’t something else going on behind it. The bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job all have ways to put pressure on me, and it’s like they’ve been taking turns tightening it until I’m supposed to give up and leave my house.\n\nSo my reasoning is not just one thing. It’s the timing, the way things connect, the way explanations are always too neat, and the way everyone keeps trying to make me ignore what I’ve been watching with my own eyes. No amount of paperwork changes that for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief similar to this one—where you felt certain that multiple people or organizations were working against you—in any other situation outside of your house and job issues?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of this. That’s what I’m trying to explain, and I know I keep coming back to it, but it’s because people keep trying to make it sound like I’m just someone who thinks groups of people are always plotting against me. I don’t walk around thinking that about random things or other parts of my life.\n\nThis is about the house, the foreclosure, and what’s been happening at work around the same time. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if the bank or the loan servicer shows me enough documents, then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s the situation where I feel certain.\n\nSo, no, I can’t point to some totally separate situation where I believed other organizations were working against me. It’s this one. But with this one, no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I mean… yes, that’s how it feels to me. I know how that probably sounds to you, like I’m saying I’m not open to anything, but it’s not that I’m trying to be stubborn. It’s that the paperwork doesn’t answer what I’ve actually been seeing.

There are people at my job who have been involved too, quietly, behind the scenes.\n\nThis has been going on for almost two years now, and every time I try to explain it, someone pulls out a document or says, “See, this is just foreclosure paperwork,” like I’m supposed to ignore everything I’ve been seeing. But papers can be made to look official. That doesn’t change the way the timing lines up, the way people act around me, the little comments, the way things keep happening one after another. I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. They show me forms and notices and say it’s just missed payments or normal foreclosure steps, but I’ve been watching how this has unfolded for almost two years. The timing, the way things happen right after I speak up, the way people act like they already know something before I’ve even said anything—it’s not normal.\n\nSo are there outside groups? I don’t know how to name it like that. I just know it doesn’t feel contained to one office or one department. It feels coordinated. And when people keep insisting it’s all just routine paperwork, that honestly makes me more sure they’re trying to make me ignore what’s right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like someone is putting thoughts into your head or controlling your mind in some way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like thoughts are being put into my head, or like somebody has a remote control over my mind or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had people try to influence me, definitely—pressure me, confuse me, make me second-guess what I know I saw. That’s not really how I would describe what’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain events, comments, or actions by others have a special or hidden meaning that’s directed specifically at you, even if others might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’ll be right after I’ve made a call, or after I’ve questioned something, or after some new paperwork shows up. They won’t say it directly, of course. That’s the whole point. It’s always indirect enough that if I repeat it, people can say, “Oh, they didn’t mean anything by that.”\n\nBut I’ve been living with this for almost two years. I’m the one seeing the pattern. I’m the one noticing how things line up. So yes, sometimes comments or little actions do feel like they’re meant for me, like a signal or a reminder that they know what’s going on and they’re still moving forward with it. Other people might not see it, but they’re not the ones it’s directed at.\n\nDoctor: Outside of what’s happening with your house and job, have you noticed events, comments, or actions by others that you felt had a special meaning directed at you?\n\nPatient: Outside of the house and my job? I mean… that’s hard to separate, because the house and the job are where most of it shows up. That’s where the pressure is coming from, or at least that’s where I can actually point to things and say, “This happened, then that happened.” I’m not saying every random person or every little thing in the world is about me.\n\nBut I’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me another letter or another form that’s supposed to erase what I’ve been seeing. And it doesn’t. The bank, the loan servicer, and even certain people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. That’s what I keep trying to explain.\n\nSo if you mean do I think, like, a billboard or a stranger’s comment at the store is sending me a message—no, not really, not in that way. But the things connected to the foreclosure, the calls, the notices, the way people at work act or say things around the same time—that has meaning. It’s directed at me. I’ve been watching it for almost two years, and everyone keeps reducing it to “routine process,” but that’s not what it feels like when you’re the one living through it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that events, actions, or comments not related to your house or job were directed at you or had a special meaning for you?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of that, no. I mean, I keep trying to say it’s not like I think everything in the world is aimed at me. It’s not strangers on the street or the news or something like that. It’s specifically what’s been happening with the house, and then how certain people at work seem tied into it.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough letters or forms then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I imagined the rest of it.” But I didn’t. I know the bank and the loan servicer have been working together, quietly, and people at my job have been involved too, to push me out of my house. And this has been going on for almost two years, so it’s not one comment or one notice that I misunderstood.\n\nSo if you’re asking about things totally unrelated to my house or my job, no, I don’t really have examples of that. That’s the part people keep ignoring.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that media, technology, or public messages (like on TV, radio, or online) were being used to send you hidden messages or communicate about you in a way that others wouldn't notice?\n\nPatient: No, not like TV or radio sending me coded messages, if that’s what you mean. I don’t sit there watching the news thinking they’re talking about me personally, or that some online ad is secretly meant for me. That’s not really what this is.\n\nWhat I’m saying is much more specific, and I feel like that keeps getting blurred. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. But the people involved in the house situation, yes, absolutely. And no amount of papers they put in front of me changes what I’ve watched happen for almost two years.\n\nDoctor: Has this pattern of feeling targeted or receiving hidden messages happened in other areas of your life before, or is this something that’s only come up around the situation with your house and job?\n\nPatient: It’s really only come up around the house and then the job because of how the job got mixed into it. I’m not saying this is some lifelong thing where I’ve always thought people were sending me signs or targeting me in every part of my life. That’s not how it started, and that’s not how I experience it.\n\nBut with this foreclosure, everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just paperwork, like missed payments and standard notices and forms, and if they show me enough pages then I’m supposed to ignore the rest of what’s been happening. I can’t do that. I know what I’ve been watching for almost two years. The bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job have been moving together quietly to push me out of my house. It’s the timing, the way things get said, the way the pressure builds from different directions and then everyone acts like it’s unrelated.\n\nSo no, not other areas before this. It’s this situation. But it’s not “just” the house in the simple way people keep saying, because once my job became part of it, it started affecting everything around me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that others might consider completely impossible or not based in reality at all, even outside of what’s happening with your house and job?\n\nPatient: No, not outside of that. I mean, I know how it sounds when I say the bank and the loan servicer and people at my job are all connected in this, because people look at me like I’m making some huge leap. But to me that is not “impossible.” That’s the part I keep trying to explain.\n\nI don’t believe in things that can’t happen, like, I don’t think there are supernatural forces or secret messages from the TV or anything like that. This is about real people and real institutions that have power over my life. Banks work with servicers. Employers talk to people. People can coordinate quietly without putting it in writing. That’s not impossible.\n\nWhat upsets me is that everyone keeps reducing it to foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, like if they stack enough documents in front of me then I’m supposed to pretend I didn’t notice the timing, the pressure, the way certain things at work started lining up with what was happening with the house. I’ve been seeing this for almost two years. So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve believed something completely outside reality. No, I don’t think so. I mean, yes, I feel certain. And I know you’re saying “even though others disagree,” but that’s the problem—everyone keeps acting like if enough people disagree with me, or if the bank sends enough paperwork, then that makes what I’m seeing disappear. It doesn’t.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps trying to make this foreclosure sound like it’s just missed payments and forms and routine notices, like there’s nothing else going on. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been watching the way it all connects for almost two years. The timing isn’t random. The pressure from the house side and then the pressure at work—it lines up too much.\n\nSo yes, when they show me papers, I look at them, but those papers don’t explain everything. They don’t explain the way people started acting, or how things kept happening at just the right time to corner me. I’m not saying this lightly. I know what I’ve seen, and no amount of documents changes that for me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe any specific evidence or reasoning that has led you to maintain your belief, even when others have shown you paperwork or explanations that suggest otherwise?\n\nPatient: It’s the pattern. That’s the evidence to me. Everyone keeps wanting one single piece of paper, one email, one person admitting it, and I’m saying that’s not how something like this would work. People don’t put “we’re coordinating to push her out” in writing. They use procedures, timing, little pressures from different directions, and then when you point it out they say, “No, that’s just normal.”\n\nI’m here because people keep acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if the bank or the servicer shows a notice or a statement then that’s supposed to settle the whole thing. But those papers don’t explain what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. They don’t explain how the pressure from the bank and loan servicer kept lining up with things happening at work, or how people at my job started acting differently right when the house situation got worse. It felt coordinated. It still does.\n\nAnd I know people say, “Well, the documents show this,” but documents can show one part and hide another. A paper can say a payment was late or a notice was sent, fine, but that doesn’t mean there isn’t something else going on behind it. The bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job all have ways to put pressure on me, and it’s like they’ve been taking turns tightening it until I’m supposed to give up and leave my house.\n\nSo my reasoning is not just one thing. It’s the timing, the way things connect, the way explanations are always too neat, and the way everyone keeps trying to make me ignore what I’ve been watching with my own eyes. No amount of paperwork changes that for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief similar to this one—where you felt certain that multiple people or organizations were working against you—in any other situation outside of your house and job issues?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside of this. That’s what I’m trying to explain, and I know I keep coming back to it, but it’s because people keep trying to make it sound like I’m just someone who thinks groups of people are always plotting against me. I don’t walk around thinking that about random things or other parts of my life.\n\nThis is about the house, the foreclosure, and what’s been happening at work around the same time. I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if the bank or the loan servicer shows me enough documents, then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s the situation where I feel certain.\n\nSo, no, I can’t point to some totally separate situation where I believed other organizations were working against me. It’s this one. But with this one, no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nDoctor: Even though others have shown you paperwork and explained this as a standard process, do you feel that no amount of evidence could ever convince you that your belief about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you is wrong?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, that’s how it feels to me. It’s that the paperwork doesn’t answer what I’ve actually been seeing.\n\nEveryone keeps bringing it back to, “Here’s the notice, here’s the missed payment, here’s the standard process,” and I understand what they’re saying on the surface. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. I do believe that. I don’t know how else to say it without people acting like I’m exaggerating, but yes, I think the bank, the loan servicer, and people at my job have been working together in a planned way to put pressure on me and push me out of my house.

And it’s not like I woke up one day and just decided that. This has been building for almost two years. Everyone keeps reducing it to “foreclosure paperwork” or “missed payments,” like if they show me another notice or statement, then that should make me drop it. But the papers don’t explain the timing. They don’t explain how things with the bank and the servicer would happen, and then things at work would line up in a way that made everything harder at the same time.

So yes, I think it’s deliberate. Quiet, maybe not obvious to everyone, but coordinated. They’re using the process and their positions to control the situation and wear me down until I leave my house. And no amount of papers they put in front of me changes what I’ve been seeing.
